10 2026 Movies That Are Officially Oscar Contenders

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

This year, the record-breaking MCU movie Spider-Man: Brand New Day and Christopher Nolan's historical epic The Odyssey headlined the biggest summer of cinema since the COVID-19 pandemic. But the late summer nights are turning to cold winter evenings, and the attention of cinephiles has turned to the biggest event in the film calendar: the Academy Awards.

The glitz and glamor of Oscars night is coming into view, as the 99th ceremony readies to celebrate the best of the past twelve months of film. Scheduled to take place March 14, 2027, at the Dolby Theatre in Los Angeles, next year's Academy Awards will feature all the brightest stars in Hollywood today, with ambitious blockbusters, indie darlings, and even a documentary hoping to earn a coveted Best Picture nomination. But who are the current favorites? Here's a look at 10 2026 movies that are officially Oscar contenders.

The film on everyone's lips, A24's You Can See Everything, might finally make history as the first documentary to earn a Best Picture nomination in 100 years of Academy history. The documentary, co-directed by Nathan Fielder and Lance Oppenheim, explores the days leading up to the incarceration of the infamous Theranos founder, Elizabeth Holmes.

"The year's most original movie will make you question reality," wrote Collider's Samuel R. Murrian in one of countless glowing reviews for this mind-bending documentary. With its viral success helping rocket the film up the box office charts, and with the world stunned by the life-altering contents of this three-hour masterpiece, it's possible that You Can See Everything ​​​​​​won't just earn a Best Picture nomination; it could win the trophy.

One of the defining films of 2026, it would be difficult to find someone who hasn't yet seen Project Hail Mary. This epic Phil Lord and Christopher Miller-directed space story follows Ryan Gosling's Ryland Grace as he leads a one-man mission into the stars to try and save the universe. However, he isn't alone in the dense emptiness of space, as he is joined on his mission by an alien he names Rocky.

A favorite to earn plenty of nominations in technical categories, with lead puppeteer and voice actor James Ortiz potentially in the race for Best Supporting Actor, Project Hail Mary will most definitely make its mark on next year's Oscars. As one of the most critically and commercially successful films of 2026, come March 14, 2027, the world could watch Grace Rocky save Oscars.

Another of the most popular films of the year, Focus Features' Obsession will hope to follow in the footsteps of The Substance and Weapons by becoming this year's horror darling at the Academy Awards. Star Inde Navarrette seems most likely to take home Oscars gold, as she currently ranks as one of the favorites in the Best Actress category for a performance that Collider's Hannah Hunt called "one of the year's best" in her review.

A box office record-breaker, becoming the highest-grossing original horror movie of this century among many more titles, Obsession shocked the world as it overcame the latest Star Wars movie through sheer viral appeal. Curry Barker's breakout is nothing short of a horror masterpiece, as one of the most important visionaries in Hollywood's next era gets off to a flying start.

Although some titles lead the charge during the Oscars race, others perform better under the radar, lurking beneath the competition before surprising cinephiles with several nominations. Right now, it seems like one of the best outside contenders for Academy recognition is Olivia Wilde's The Invite, a remake of the Spanish-language comedy The People Upstairs.

Unfolding like a great play, The Invite takes a simple premise, a single location, and four actors at the top of their game and transforms them into an utterly unmissable comedy. But this comedy isn't without its dramatic flair, with every twist and turn playing out like a hilariously realized thriller. Starring Seth Rogen, Edward Norton, and Penélope Cruz alongside Wilde, The Invite feels destined to earn recognition in writing and acting categories.

If, for the aforementioned Penélope Cruz, Oscar night doesn't equal gold for Wilde's The Invite, rest assured she stands a second chance as part of the ensemble of one of the bookies' favorites. Javier Calvo and Javier Ambrossi's La Bola Negra is a 155-minute drama inspired by the unfinished work of the same name that explores the connections between three gay men and the pain they endure.

Themes of love and loss in the queer community, as well as the erasure of queer identity, become ever more relevant each passing year, with these topics better represented at the Academy Awards in the process. La Bola Negra is the latest LGBTQIA+ story to receive the Oscars spotlight, as it is deemed a favorite to not just earn a Best Picture nomination, but to win the prize.

When forecasting the Oscars nominees for 2027, it's wise to look back at the historical voting of the often predictable Academy. Both 2021's Dune and 2022's Dune: Part Two received nominations in the Best Picture category, having combined to win eight trophies so far. So, with that in mind, the final installment in Denis Villeneuve's acclaimed trilogy seems destined to pick up a handful of nominations once again.

The ambitious attempt to turn the once-thought "unfilmable" Frank Herbert story into cinematic magic has already been the subject of endless praise from critics. But in his last installment, perhaps it's time for Villeneuve to win in the biggest categories? That's what many sci-fi fans will think, and as December 18 and the consequences of Paul Atreides' (Timothée Chalamet) power inch closer, whispers of a Dune: Part Three Best Picture bid grow.

Andrew Scott has quietly proven to be one of the most consistent actors working today, submitting a fine selection of truly terrific performances over the past few years. Come Oscars night next year, it seems the Sherlock star will finally receive recognition as one of the favorites to take home the Best Actor prize as Ian Charleson in the stunning drama, Elsinore.

Directed by Simon Stone and written by Stephen Beresford, Elsinore looks likely to be the second Hamlet-related film in two years to take home an acting prize at the Oscars, following Jessie Buckley's deserved win for Hamnet. Also like Hamnet, Elsinore looks to be this year's biggest tearjerker, with Collider's Therese Lacson writing, "For god's sake, bring more than just one pack of tissues to the theater," in her review.

In 2023, The Banshees of Inisherin was one of the year's biggest nominees, having earned nods in eight categories. Sadly, it won zero prizes, a fate that Irish filmmaker Martin McDonagh will be hoping to avoid with his latest Academy Award play, Wild Horse Nine. The film follows two CIA agents on a mission from Santiago to Easter Island in 1973, and is packed with all of McDonagh's typical dark, comedic wit.

As usual, a McDonagh movie looks set to take home nominations in writing categories, with star John Malkovich heavily tipped to take on the aforementioned Scott for Best Actor. Sam Rockwell, Steve Buscemi, Parker Posey, and the rest of the supporting cast deserve just as much credit for this uniquely encapsulating effort from an unstoppable filmmaker.

It's rare that any given year's Palme d'Or winner doesn't make an impact at the Academy Awards. In recent years, the likes of Bong Joon Ho's Parasite and Sean Baker's Anora have won both Cannes' biggest prize and the Best Picture trophy, so it's fair to assume that Cristian Mungiu's Fjord will also make its presence known come Oscar nominations day.

Mungiu's second Palme d'Or winner is arguably his most controversial film, touching on deeply nuanced topics and never offering an easy moral way out. But it is in this nuance that Fjord thrives, with Sebastian Stan and Renate Reinsve delivering perfectly detailed performances that capture both the best and worst of these troubled characters and their terrifying situation.

The front-runner to take home the Academy's biggest prize is Christopher Nolan's historical epic The Odyssey, dubbed an "unrivaled cinematic spectacle" in Joe Schmidt's review for Collider. This famous tale's star-studded retelling hasn't been without its detractors, but those with a say have made it clear their love for The Odyssey, placing it firmly at the #1 spot in many people's predictions.

In fact, The Odyssey could very well overtake Ryan Coogler's Sinners as the most nominated film of all time, considering its sure impact in both the major and craft categories. Nolan delivers his typically ambitious scale and scope to one of history's grandest stories, culminating in a final thirty minutes that are, without doubt, some of the most encapsulating cinema of the decade so far. Could The Odyssey sail to a record-breaking Oscars night? All will be revealed in a few months.

Sunday Night Football: Bears overcome five giveaways, beat Bucs 27-24

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

It was a wild one in Chicago, but the Bears were able to pull out a 27-24 victory over the Buccaneers and move to 7-2 on the season.

Caleb Williams and the Bears’ offense started out white hot, but an interception midway through the second quarter threw off the unit for nearly the rest of the night. Williams ended up tossing four interceptions — the most in a single game he’s had in his college or pro career.

But even with five giveaways — Luther Burden also had a fumble in the fourth quarter — the Buccaneers scored just 10 points off of them. Tampa Bay couldn’t turn a blocked punt into points either, despite getting the ball at midfield.

Chicago began the game with a 14-0 lead, as Williams tossed touchdowns to Rome Odunze and Burden. The Buccaneers got on the board with Chase McLaughlin’s 27-yard field goal in the second quarter before Cairo Santos hit from 25 yards to make the score 17-3 with 3:02 left in the half. McLaughlin drilled a 21-yard field goal to end the half, with Bucs head coach Todd Bowles explaining in his halftime interview on the NBC broadcast that he felt his team could score to start the third quarter.

That turned into reality, as Bucky Irving scored a 2-yard touchdown to cap Tampa Bay’s opening drive in the second half, narrowing Chicago’s lead to 17-13.

While Williams threw his fourth interception, Baker Mayfield then threw a pick of his own to Jaylon Johnson, who returned it 67 yards for a touchdown.

McLaughlin again made it a one-possession game with a 35-yard field goal with 11:24 in the fourth quarter. Then Burden’s fumble gave the Buccaneers the opportunity to tie it deep in Bears territory, with Irving scoring on a 6-yard run. Mayfield made the two-point conversion successful by scrambling up the middle to the end zone.

But even with the offensive challenges, the Bears got the play they needed to win it. Williams connected with Colton Loveland for a 38-yard pass all the way down to the Tampa Bay 16.

After running time off the clock, Santos sent the ball through the uprights as the fourth quarter expired for a 38-yard, game-winning field goal.

Williams finished the contest 22-of-37 for 279 yards with two touchdowns and four picks. Burden led with seven catches for 97 yards with a TD. He had to be examined in the sideline medical tent after his fumble in the fourth quarter and was subsequently taken back to the locker room for further evaluation.

Mayfield finished 18-of-32 for 243 yards with two picks. Irving had 97 yards on 20 carries with two TDs, plus four catches for 28 yards.

With the win, the Bears are 7-2 in the league’s best division — as all teams in the NFC North have at least six wins. Chicago will be on its bye in Week 10 before hosting the Saints in Week 11.

At 3-6, the Buccaneers will also be on their bye in Week 10. They’ll look to get back on track on the road against the Lions in Week 11.

Linus Torvalds sees signs AI-prompted patch flood has peaked

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Fixes are arriving faster than new bug reports for one major Linux project

The worst of the patchpocalypse may be behind us, according to Linus Torvalds.

Writing in his weekly kernel update post, the Linux kernel dev leader delivered Linux 7.4 rc2 – the second rough cut of the next kernel – which he rated as a big release for two reasons.

One is that he left some code out of rc1, because he was busy and because it was already the largest such release in the history of the kernel.

The other is what Torvalds described as “various AI fixes.”

Such fixes are necessary because ever since Anthropic launched its Mythos bug-finding model in April, developers have been able to find more software flaws than was previously possible.

Microsoft, for example, addressed a record 974 flaws in September – its third record fixing spree this year after it whacked 622 flaws in July and 206 in June. Oracle addressed 1,449 flaws in a single month.

That’s many more patches than IT teams are set up to handle, yet they must find ways to deal with bugs more quickly because attackers are increasingly targeting flaws found by AI.

Torvalds’ post suggests that, perhaps, the worst of this is behind us.

“This time around Alexei claims that on the bpf side at least the fixes are now coming in faster than the new reports, and that sounds lovely if true,” he wrote.

That’s a reference to Alexei Starovoitov, co-creator and lead dev on the Extended Berkeley Packet Filter (eBPF), the project that makes it possible to run sandboxed programs within the Linux kernel.

“I think most other areas haven't reached that point yet, but it's at least a sign that we _may_ be starting to see the tide starting to turn,” he added. “Don't hold your breath, but maybe cross your fingers we'll be getting there in the not entirely distant future.”

If Torvalds is correct, that’s welcome news. The Linux kernel’s 40-million-plus lines of code makes it one of the world’s bigger software projects. If AI soon stops finding flaws in other parts of the kernel beyond eBPF, perhaps the deluge of fixes developers and users alike will need to handle has peaked. ®

10 Most Perfect Thriller Movies of All Time

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

One simply can't get enough of thriller movies. Fast-paced, heart-pounding, and packed with twists and surprises, the genre feeds off the reactions of audiences as they are pulled to the edge of their seats. A good thriller delivers suspense, but an excellent one reinvents classic thriller elements, all while staying relevant to the anxieties of the era it was released in.

Whether it's the effects of corporate culture, a rookie police officer taking on 30 levels of criminals, or a chef who's sick and tired of serving uppity patrons, these movies show that there's more than one way to deliver the adrenaline. That said, here are 10 intense thriller movies that are perfect from start to finish.

What happens when a revolutionary loses his spark? One Battle After Another introduces us to Bob Ferguson (Leonardo DiCaprio), a former demolition expert for a militant group who, 16 years later, has become a jaded single father to the teenage Willa (Chase Infiniti). But when a secret is revealed about Willa’s true origins, she goes off the grid, prompting the washed-up Bob to reignite the fire he once had.

One Battle After Another has the makings of a classic thriller. However, there is a refreshing comedic edge to him. Considering that he’s not as energetic and physically limber as he used to be, he falls and stumbles as he evades the cops. Bob can barely run without losing his breath, but with his daughter kidnapped, he’s not catching a break anytime soon.

Sinners refuses to be boxed into conventional thriller norms. When twin brothers Smoke and Stack (Michael B. Jordan) open a massive juke joint on the outskirts of town, the last thing they expect is for a group of vampires to crash the party. As the night wears on, these undead intruders begin turning the brothers’ human guests one by one.

Historical, breathtaking, and full of movement, Ryan Coogler has a knack for using horror to bring up broader themes like racial identity and systemic oppression. In Sinners, blues music serves as the voice of the marginalized, giving power to those who play it. Coogler goes one step further by portraying blues as a near-magical force that can literally open portals to the supernatural.

No other thriller has tested an audience’s perception of reality more than Oldboy. For reasons unknown, a drunken Oh Dae-su (Choi Min-sik) is abducted and imprisoned in a sealed room with no communication to the outside world. 15 years have passed, and the now disoriented Dae-su is suddenly returned to society — this time framed for the murder of his wife.

It’s mystery after mystery in Oldboy, but it all boils down to a hyper-specific revenge ploy. Sometimes, death is too swift and merciful a solution — although the movie does boast graphic scenes like the violent hallway fight. Instead, the most effective kind of revenge is one that twists a person’s cognition, leading them to make immoral decisions by their own doing.

Corporate gets cold-blooded in American Psycho. Although modern movies like The Wolf of Wall Street show the high-adrenaline rush from working in investment, Christian Bale’s performance as banker Patrick Bateman is much more polished and restrained — but even more deadly. A swanky apartment and a ridiculous salary aren’t enough to fill his empty void, prompting him to find alternative sources of pleasure.

Thus begins the true thesis of American Psycho — the rich aren’t devoid of feeling because they lack material wealth; it’s because they can’t appreciate what they have. Capitalism emphasizes the idea of having more. Unfortunately, the more Bateman tries to gain — whether status, money, or women — the less satisfaction he finds, until he finds something that cannot be bought: the ability to control whether someone lives or dies.

The only thing that’s separating a 20-man SWAT team and one crime lord in The Raid is a 30-story rundown apartment. But like Dante’s nine circles of hell, each level in the building is infested with all sorts of criminals. From drug dealers to machete-wielding killers, rookie officer Rama must conquer the threats on each floor before getting his hands on the feared underworld kingpin, Tama Riyadi.

There is a non-stop, relentless pace that drives The Raid from start to finish. The not-so-fun part about raiding a high-rise building is that there is practically no way of escaping. The hallways are tight and narrow, meaning that any criminal could just jump on Rama from whichever direction, amplifying the anxiety from being only literally one step away from being stabbed by a knife.

There's no knowing where a Quentin Tarantino thriller might go, and the in-your-face Inglourious Basterds is as unpredictable as they come. Lt. Aldo Raine (Brad Pitt) is responsible for his "Basterds," a crew of Jewish-American soldiers whose job is to infiltrate Nazi spaces and kill them. Somewhere in the French countryside, Shosanna Dreyfus (Mélanie Laurent) has a personal vendetta against the infamous "Jew Hunter," Hans Landa (Christoph Waltz).

Inglourious Basterds doesn’t skimp on the anxiety of being hunted like helpless prey, and much of that tension comes from Walt's performance as Landa. Widely regarded as one of cinema’s greatest villains, Waltz plays him as chillingly polite one moment and completely ruthless the next, especially when he suspects he has found a Jew in his presence.

In Parasite, the resourceful Ki-taek (Song Kang-ho) does everything he can to pull his Kim family out of their scrappy, cramped basement apartment. His luck changes when his son, Ki-woo (Choi Woo-shik), secures a tutoring position with the affluent Park family. With no proper qualifications, he sweet-talks his way into the job, gradually bringing the rest of his family into the Parks’ lives.

Parasite challenges the audience’s pre-existing notions about the rich versus the poor. While it may seem unethical for the Kim family to manipulate the Parks, they are simply doing what they can to achieve upward mobility. The Parks, on the other hand, appear kind because they have the resources to afford generosity. The film suggests that privilege itself enables the ability to be “nice.”

A weekend out of the city goes sideways in Get Out. Black photographer Chris Washington (Daniel Kaluuya) accepts an invitation from his white girlfriend, Rose Armitage (Allison Williams), to a family gathering at her home in the countryside. Unbeknownst to him, her parents have a horrifying pastime of hypnotizing and lobotomizing Black individuals as a vessel for immortality.

Get Out reimagines how modern-day slavery would take place. The scariest part is that, unlike in the past, with recorded accounts of white masters being viciously cruel, Rose’s family initially welcomes Chris with open arms. This sense of niceness — almost too nice — strikes a nerve, considering that Chris is the only Black person in the countryside with no outside communication.

Unlike Marco Pierre White and Gordon Ramsay, Chef Julian Slowik (Ralph Fiennes) is cool as a cucumber — the perfect facade to hide his taste for revenge in The Menu. Obnoxious food snob Tyler (Nicholas Hoult) takes his less-than-enthusiastic date Margot (Anya Taylor-Joy) to a group fine dining experience at Chef Julian's secluded island restaurant, only for the night to get bloody.

Despite the terror they face from the disillusioned Chef Julian, there’s something darkly funny about paying $1,250 per person only to end up getting killed. Then again, The Menu is about a service industry man’s attempt to get revenge on the entitled guests who have caused him to lose his passion for the simple joy of cooking. The wealthy will pay anything for an “experience,” and that is exactly what they’re going to get.

Nobody can trust anyone in The Handmaiden. Set in Japanese-occupied Korea in the 1930s, Sook-hee (Kim Tae-ri) is appointed as the loyal handmaiden to the Japanese heiress, Lady Hideko. Unlike the tales of cruel masters, Lady Hideko (Kim Min-hee) shows nothing but compassion to Sook-hee. Unbeknownst to them, the male powers shadowing over them plan to use them as pawns for material and colonial gains.

The Handmaiden is as much of a period piece and a forbidden romance as it is a thriller. Considering the context of the time, the two leading women, despite their class differences, are no less put into precarious positions. For all its sickening plot twists, the bond between Sook-he and Lady Hideko gives the thriller a strong sense of heart. After all, the two are only trying to escape from their oppression, even if that route requires manipulation.

Thank you for subscribing to our newsletter,

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

The best of Collider, directly in your inbox.

Stephen King’s Supernatural Sci-Fi Series Is Officially Back, And It’s The Perfect Carrie Follow-Up

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Mike Flanagan's Carrie became the biggest Stephen King hit of the year, and it is all set to be followed up with season 2. However, it will be a while before Carrie season 2 sees the light of day. While waiting for its release, horror fans can now watch another ongoing TV adaptation of a Stephen King book, which has officially returned in 2026.

The show in question, The Institute, has a lot in common with Carrie, and these parallels between the two Stephen King stories will now be even more evident.

Carrie marked Stephen King's literary debut. The Institute, in contrast, ranks among the King of Horror's more recent books as it was published in 2019. Its first TV adaptation of the same name landed on MGM+ and garnered a more mixed reception compared to recent shows like It: Welcome to Derry and Carrie. Still, MGM+'s The Institute performed well enough to be renewed for another season.

Interestingly, The Institute season 1 exhausted the story from the original Stephen King novel. Owing to this, its second season is now expanding the source material's lore. After season 1's developments and season 2's opening arcs, The Institute is already starting to look like the perfect follow-up to Stephen King's Carrie. The two Stephen King stories also share some hidden connections.

Carrie was the first breakout horror novel in which Stephen King used telekinesis as a narrative device to highlight the impact of abuse on a young adult. The Institute not only riffs on the same themes but also significantly raises the stakes. Instead of featuring only one character with the "Shine," it shows how multiple children are gifted with telekinesis (TK), telepathy (TP), and precognition.

The characters in The Institute, however, are subjected to explicit institutional abuse after being abducted from their homes and subjected to immoral experimentation. It almost feels like a sequel to Carrie where the White Commission has realized that more children like Carrie White exist. Now, they have started an "institute" to exploit their powers.

The Institute season 2's episode 1 further leans into these overlaps. It shows how entire systems have been built to exploit children with superpowers. The episode introduces older test subjects who use their abilities to help an organization serve a perceived greater good. This introduction of older subjects suggests that humans with TK and TP have been around for a very long time.

Its origins can probably be traced back to Carrie White.

Season 2's episode 1 also reveals how the overarching corporate-backed project is trying to develop an AI that could replace humans with precognition, TK, and TP. However, AI is just not as effective as humans. Mike Flanagan's Carrie also ends with the titular character surviving, paving the way for season 2.

It is hard not to see how her story will be similar to the young characters from The Institute who are struggling to escape the clutches of a powerful shadow organization.

Stephen King never draws any explicit connections between The Dark Tower, Carrie, and The Institute. However, it is hard not to notice many clear overlaps between all three. For instance, in The Dark Tower universe, all psychic abilities are manifestations of a single cosmic force called the "Shine."

In The Dark Tower book series, specifically in Wolves of the Calla and The Dark Tower, characters with psychic powers are called are held captive by the Crimson King and referred to as Breakers. The Breakers are collectively expected to use their powers and chip away at the Beams of reality. Even the characters in The Institute are expected to do the same through quantum manipulation.

Put simply, in the grand scheme of Stephen King's larger horror universe, Carrie tells the foundational story of young adults with superpowers. The Institute and Firestarter step things up by featuring organizations that exploit these powers. Finally, The Dark Tower walks through the cosmic purpose of these powers, revealing why those with the "Shine" are so sought after in every Stephen King story.

Carrie serves as the first step for Mike Flanagan to establish a larger Stephen King universe on the small screen. He will double down on these connections and overlaps in Carrie season 2 and his highly-anticipated Dark Tower adaptation.

The Institute, released in 2025, follows 14-year-old genius Luke Ellis, who is kidnapped and wakes up at a mysterious facility filled with children possessing unusual abilities. Meanwhile, Tim Jamieson, a former police officer, seeks a fresh start in a nearby town, inevitably intertwining his fate with Luke's.

At Last, The MCU Is Officially Setting Up The Best Doctor Doom/Scarlet Witch Story

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Marvel Studios is preparing for the debut of Robert Downey Jr.s Doctor Doom in Avengers: Doomsday, which is set to release at the end of this year. However, an exciting new theory suggests Doom's arrival in the MCU may also involve the return of The Scarlet Witch.

Played by Elizabeth Olsen, Wanda Maximoff's Scarlet Witch has been absent from the main MCU continuity since 2022's Doctor Strange in the Multiverse of Madness, which saw Wanda controversially being corrupted by the Darkhold in a bid to reunite with her sons from a different reality (no matter the cost). Eventually, Wanda was shown how far she'd fallen, prompting her to sacrifice herself by bringing down Mount Wundagore, destroying every copy of the Darkhold in the process.

That said, many fans have never stopped believing in Wanda's eventual return to the MCU, especially with the rest of her family still having such prominent, ongoing roles. Between the new VisionQuest series on Disney+ and the reveal of mysterious mystical characters beyond Victor von Doom in Avengers: Doomsday, there's plenty reason to suspect that Marvel Studios has secret plans for the Scarlet Witch (which may involve Doctor Doom himself).

Beginning with 2021's WandaVision, Wanda's grief losing Vision (Paul Bettany) in Avengers: Infinity War led to her inadvertent creation of a massive hex over the town of Westview, transforming it into her TV-perfect happy ending, while also manifesting a new Vision and their twin boys from the comics, Billy and Tommy. By the end of WandaVision, Wanda undid the hex despite the fact it meant losing Vision and here boys. This is what ultimately led to her corruption by the Darkhold trying to get her boys back via another reality and subsequent death at Mount Wundagore in 2022's Multiverse of Madness.

However, the story of Wanda Maximoff's family has very much continued even without Wanda herself. In 2024's Agatha All Along, it was revealed that Billy used his magic to transfer his soul into the body of teenager William Kaplan (Joe Locke) before the Hex was being undone. He then did the same for Tommy's soul with Thomas Shepherd (Ruaridh Mollica), a key parallel to their origins from the comics.

While Agatha All Along ended with Billy/William and the ghost of Agatha Harkness searching for Tommy/Thomas, we now have 2026's VisionQuest starring the original Vision (rebuilt by SWORD at the end of WandaVision), as well as Tommy himself who's gradually been learning from his synthezoid "father" about Wanda, the events of Westview, and their family.

With VisionQuest just days away from its finale, Vision and the Maximoff twins finding each other again feels increasingly likely. Combined with Vision starting to regain the humanity and emotion he temporarily lost after being reactivated, nearly all of the pieces are in place for a major Maximoff family reunion in the MCU's future except one: Wanda.

While she seemed to have died under the collapse of Mount Wundagore, The Scarlet Witch is one of the most powerful beings in the entire Marvel Universe. As such, it's not much of a leap in logic to suggest that she could have survived (or was perhaps even rescued by someone seeking her power...)

Beyond what's been happening on the small-screen for the surviving Maximoffs, the ambiguity and hopes about Wanda's return have never been higher thanks to Avengers: Doomsday and its masked "Latverian Witches" who will apparently be serving Doctor Doom.

Their exact identities and purpose of these witches in the upcoming MCU crossover have yet to be confirmed, and they may very well just be generic servants of Doom (a master sorcerer in his own right). However, they've notably been shown wearing different colored robes including green, purple, orange, and red, leading many to wonder if established magical characters like Sylvie, Agatha, Clea, or Wanda herself could be under Doom's influence.

Choose the movie with the higher Rotten Tomatoes score.

Sign in to claim your place on the leaderboard!

Thinking about Wanda in particular, Doom would have certainly had the power to secretly intervene to save Wanda at Mount Wundagore, perhaps in a bid to bring her into his service and plans for the multiverse at large. There's also some key comics precedent for this idea as well, as seen in Marvel's classic "Children's Crusade" storyline.

In "Children's Crusade", Doctor Doom secretly takes in an amnesiac Wanda from Mount Wundagore after her major breakdown that resulted in her magic triggering the near-genocide of mutantkind (House of M). Hoping to marry Wanda and use her power for his own ends, the intervention of Wanda's boys, their fellow Young Avengers, and several Avengers and X-Men alike prevented Doom from taking full control of the Scarlet Witch's power and her memories being restored.

While none of this confirms that the MCU's Wanda Maximoff is indeed one of these Latverian Witches in Doomsday, being onee of the masked magic wielders would provide a very natural way for the MCU to kick-off a new storyline inspired by "Children's Crusade", perhaps even one that continues beyond the Multiverse Saga itself.

The basic premise of "Children's Crusade" feels remarkably perfect to the MCU's future. While VisionQuest can bring Billy and Tommy together with Vision, Avengers: Doomsday can establish Doom's true purpose for these Latverian Witches, and whether one of them is indeed Wanda Maximoff. Perhaps the reveal will even saved for the end of Doomsday or even in 2027's Avengers: Secret Wars to then kick off a substantial project in the MCU's next major saga.

Most importantly, this major MCU thoery would go a long way to redeem the events of Multiverse of Madness and Wanda's controversial (and underveloped) turn to the darkness. Likewise, throwing Doctor Doom into the mix even just to start would not only keep the Maximoff Family's story tied to the greater events happening in the MCU, but would also provide the perfect narrative launchpad by adapting the basic elements from "Children's Crusade".

It may be some time before we get it, but the twins' reunion more than likely approaching and Doctor Doom's mysterious magical followers definitely makes it feel like a solid foundation is being formed for Wanda's long hoped-for return in the MCU.

The finale of VisionQuest begins streaming November 10th on Disney+. Avengers: Doomsday releases in theaters on December 18th from Marvel Studios.

Lead designer Bruce Nesmith says Skyrim was a hit because it was built on institutional knowledge from Oblivion

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Maybe laying off people who know what they're doing is bad, actually.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

I don't know about you, but I'm not a huge fan of the way YouTube encourages everything becoming a video that's over an hour long and doesn't have a text version available. There's a recent video putting the original Pool of Radiance into the greater context of the history of CRPGs that's almost 10 hours long, and the odds of me watching more than 10% of it are perishingly low.

Fortunately for you, we're here to convert video to text for your edification, as with The Examined Game's comprehensive interview with Skyrim lead designer Bruce Nesmith. Asked how a game as successful as Skyrim came to be, Nesmith emphasized the importance of keeping the team together.

If I had an actual blueprint on how to do it, I would be one rich son of a bitch. A lot of it comes down to we had the same team that had worked on Oblivion. The studio had not really changed dramatically. So we were able to deal with institutional knowledge at a high level that had been retained, which allows us to not make mistakes and build on successes. That was definitely key to it.

I think also we gave a high priority to making sure that the world was full. There was nowhere that you went where there wasn't at least something fun to see if not something fun to do. And it and it can be just little things, it can be a little vignette on the side of the road. It doesn't have to be a huge big adventure, but the visual storytelling was fantastic.

I go back to that sense of freedom once again, but we accelerated that in Skyrim compared to Oblivion. It makes the world feel rich when there's a variety of experiences. The same experience over and over and over again doesn't cut it. So you have a wealth of enemy types, you've got a lot of different treasure types and loot types, you're allowed to pick everything up in the world.

I have to disagree with Nesmith on the characters, because memorable NPCs are one of Skyrim's weak spots—all those people with recycled voices and limited dialogue. But he's right that Skyrim feels full despite the absence of Ubisoft-style repeatable activities. At no point do you pick up a butterfly and get a notification that says "1/100 butterflies collected". The butterflies are just there, and you can grab them and turn them into potion ingredients if you want. Then while you're out harvesting ingredients you meet a thief or a dying orc or an NPC who points you to a sidequest, and you're off on a new journey. That's what Skyrim does well.

Nesmith goes on to say depth like that was made possible, "by having people who knew what they were doing and could hit the ground running. You didn't have to train people what it meant to make an Elder Scrolls game." Instead of laying off half the Oblivion team the moment it shipped, Bethesda kept them together. "If you look at the history of AAA studios that do sequels, you'll see that repeated," Nesmith continues. "You'll see the best game in a sequence frequently was able to maintain a particular percentage of the staff—was able to maintain a vision."

Alice Huang, also known as Huang Le, is a Chinese citizen and former senior executive at Singapore-based data centre firm Megaspeed, which bought more than $4.8 billion worth of Nvidia chips over two years, according to the New York Times. She is not related to Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang. A Chinese private fund database lists her as a former TV reporter at Hunan TV and state broadcaster CCTV, an assistant at HSBC’s private banking arm and a vice president at CITIC Securities. The New York Post, however, could not find a CCTV profile for her, and a Hunan TV executive said he had never heard of her. She was first photographed beside Jensen Huang at a Taipei bar in June 2024. A classified memo alleges that China’s Ministry of State Security recruited her to gather information on Nvidia. These claims have not been publicly proven. President Trump called the matter very complex and said officials would look into it closely.

Oliver opined how it was “genuinely hard to believe Texas had just turned blue,” adding it was “hard to process” all the wins, including 22 state trifectas, Chris Pappas becoming the first openly-gay man elected to U.S. Senate, Deb Haaland of New Mexico being the first Native American woman elected governor of a U.S. state, and Georgia’s Keisha Lance Bottoms elected the first Black woman governor of a U.S. state.

But wait, there’s more: “Then it hits you that Elon Musk wasted so much money! The half a billion dollars he spent to try and buy the election just went up in flames faster than a parked Cybertruck. Speaking of which: we also learned this week he’s ending production of Cybertrucks, which isn’t an election result but kind of feels like one.”

Yes, Musk spent nearly $500 million during the midterm election cycle, including $200 million to (unsuccessfully) defeat Senator-elect James Talarico in Texas; while Stevens, a supposed Democrat, backed GOP candidate Mike Rogers over her primary rival (and eventual winner) El-Sayed.

Nvidia’s board has set up a special committee of independent directors, supported by outside lawyers, to review allegations involving CEO Jensen Huang. The move follows reports that Huang had a relationship with Alice Huang, a former senior executive at Singapore-based Megaspeed International, whom US intelligence agencies reportedly suspected of having ties to Beijing. Huang has backed the review and pledged full cooperation. No wrongdoing has been found. Here Are 5 Of Today's Best Gas Sport Compacts, Ranked By Horsepower

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Enthusiasts like to moan about how performance cars aren't as common or affordable as they used to be, but if you actually look at the market and account for inflation, we're sort of living through a golden age of sport compact cars right now. Toyota, Honda, Volkswagen, Subaru, and even Hyundai all make go-fast versions of their entry-level compacts. They're all scrappy, front- or all-wheel-drive machines that have hatches, trunks, and back seats, and most are still available with manual transmissions.

Some are easier to get ahold of than others, but for now, they all sticker at or under $50,000. They all make around 300 horsepower, an amount that we at Jalopnik have determined is the correct amount of horsepower. And we've lined up the best gas-powered sport compacts on the market right now (sorry, Ioniq 5 N Stans), ranking them on how many ponies their little engines put out.

To the continued disappointment of its fans, Subaru hasn't made a bona fide WRX STI since 2022, but the less powerful, current WRX still arguably lands in, or at least close enough to, the 300-horsepower ballpark. Subie's street-legal rally machine makes 271 hp out of its turbocharged 2.4-liter flat-four engine. You can get it with a six-speed manual, but also a continuously variable transmission (CVT) that the automaker euphemistically calls the Subaru Performance Transmission if you so choose.

This being a Subaru, symmetrical all-wheel drive is standard, and my brief time behind the wheel confirms that it is quite good on a loose surface. On-road, its inputs feel looser than those of its rivals, the powertrain can feel a tad agricultural, and as you'd probably assume from the numbers, it isn't quite as potent. On this list, the WRX may come last in terms of power, but if it's any consolation, it costs a good amount less than the top performers. A new base trim introduced for 2026 starts at just $33,690 (including a $1,195 destination charge).

Also keeping the affordable sport compact flame alive is the $36,595 Hyundai Elantra N. This car's turbocharged 2.0-liter four-cylinder makes 276 hp if you opt for the six-speed manual, and the power is the same most of the time with the available seven-speed dual-clutch automatic. The latter, however, comes with an "N Grin Shift" push-to-pass-style boost system that amps things up to 286 hp temporarily. That's still not enough to shift its ranking on this list, but an extra 10 hp is an extra 10 hp.

In practice, raw speed is one of the less notable aspects of the Elantra N experience. It sounds rude and burbly, its limited-slip differential, sticky tires, and huge brakes make it an absolute treat to carve up a backroad in, and despite being on the cheaper end of the spectrum, it has one of the nicer interiors in this class. Hyundai has already unveiled the new-gen Elantra, which means an N version of that is very likely around the corner, but this one is still a good deal while it lasts.

Speaking of corners, the Toyota GR Corolla is a car that absolutely loves 'em. Can't live without them, really. It's still a firecracker down the straights, though, pumping out 300 horsepower on the dot out of its 1.6-liter turbocharged three-cylinder — yes, three-cylinder. It's the only car on here that doesn't have four pistons. A six-speed manual is the standard gearbox, naturally, but Toyota added an available eight-speed auto starting from the 2025 model year.

Out of all the cars on this list, the GR Corolla feels the rowdiest to drive. The least refined, the least luxurious, and the least concerned about comfort, tech amenities, or cargo space. But that's arguably where its charm lies. That three-cylinder engine always sounds like it's about to explode, while GR-Four AWD and Torsen limited-slip diffs front and rear make it feel like the rally machine that it is, constantly. But if it's pure horsepower you're after, there are a couple of hot hatchbacks that trounce it. A 2026 Toyota GR Corolla with the manual transmission starts at $41,815.

One of the cars that trounces the GR Corolla (relatively speaking, of course) is the mighty Honda Civic Type R, which makes 315 hp out of its turbocharged 2.0-liter four-cylinder engine. Like the Elantra, power goes to the front wheels only, and the Type R has the distinction of being the only vehicle here to be offered exclusively with a manual transmission. Once you've driven it, though, that decision makes a whole lot more sense, because not only does this car have the best shifter in this class hands-down, it's one of the best shifters ever made, regardless of segment or age. Add in a weapon of a front axle, steering to die for, and a chassis that makes some of the other cars on this list feel floppy, and you have one of the best performance cars available today, period.

This car gets a mild update for 2027 with improved engine cooling, tweaked damping in +R mode, better rev-matching, and a brake pedal that's said to feel more direct. It starts at $49,990, although finding a dealer who will actually sell it to you for that number may take some effort.

As great as that Civic is, it isn't the most powerful gas-powered hot hatch available today. That honor goes to the Volkswagen Golf R, which makes 328 hp out of its turbocharged 2.0-liter, sending power to all four wheels. VW used to sell this with either a six-speed manual or a seven-speed dual-clutch auto, but as of the 2025 refresh, the Golf R is auto-only. Perhaps the same lean toward outright pace that gives it the most horsepower in its class was also responsible for it ditching the manual.

The Golf R is an undoubtedly speedy vehicle. Behind the wheel, it feels relatively composed and like a buttoned-down brand of hot hatchery, which pairs well with its very practical, luxury car-adjacent interior. On the flip side, that also means it likely won't be your first choice if you're looking for an unapologetic sense of theater or fun. But if you must have the one car in this class that'll win the most spec sheet comparisons, the VW Golf R is your guy. The 2027 Volkswagen Golf R starts at $51,320 (assuming the $1,275 destination charge carries over from 2026).

tBTC bridge partially resumes as $120M Aave reserve remains frozen

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Threshold halted its tBTC bridge on Oct. 30 as operators addressed a critical Linux kernel vulnerability that could allow attackers to execute code on unpatched servers.

Threshold Network’s tBTC bridge has resumed onchain operations after a nine-day security pause, but an Aave reserve holding about $120 million in tBTC remains frozen to new deposits and borrowing against the token.

On Sunday, Threshold said the bridge’s underlying network was restarting as security-patched node operators reconnected. These independent operators collectively authorize Bitcoin movements and process deposits and redemptions.

Requests submitted before the shutdown are expected to be processed as more operators return, but the app will not accept new deposits, minting or redemptions until enough nodes are online. Threshold did not announce a restart date.

Threshold initially halted the bridge on Oct. 30 while operators addressed a critical Linux kernel vulnerability that could allow remote attackers to execute code on unpatched servers. The project said it found no evidence the flaw was exploited against tBTC nodes.

Aave froze its tBTC reserve on Ethereum Core on Oct. 31 to prevent additional exposure while redemptions were unavailable. The restriction stops new tBTC deposits and the token from adding borrowing power, while existing users can still withdraw and repay loans.

Threshold said roughly 4,320 BTC remains in bridge wallets against about 4,185 tBTC in circulation, with no unauthorized Bitcoin transfers or token minting detected. Aave risk provider LlamaRisk said it would propose restoring the reserve after Threshold’s operations return to normal.

Why Boeing 777 Freighter Conversions Are A Stopgap Built On A Shrinking Supply Of Used Jets

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

The Boeing 777 is helping the global air cargo industry address a growing shortage of large freighters, but the aircraft providing that additional capacity are becoming very difficult to find. As passenger airlines retain their widebody jets for longer and new-build freighters face production and delivery constraints, three conversion programs are competing to turn used 777s into dedicated cargo aircraft. The irony is that the industry's immediate solution depends on an aging aircraft supply that cannot be replenished indefinitely.

For cargo operators, converted 777s offer an attractive way to obtain substantial payload capacity without waiting for a new-generation freighter. However, their fuel consumption, acquisition costs, and remaining service lives raise questions about their long-term commercial viability. With the Airbus A350F approaching entry into service and the 777-8F still in development, the converted 777 looks like a temporary answer to a problem that will require a more permanent solution.

Growing interest in Boeing 777 passenger-to-freighter (P2F) conversions is closely tied to a shortage of available large widebody freighters. Cargo airlines need more capacity, but the transition from existing production programs to next-generation aircraft has created a gap they can't fill immediately. Production constraints, certification delays, and changing emissions requirements have made used passenger aircraft an important source of additional capacity.

The 777-300ER variant is particularly attractive because of its large fuselage, substantial structural capabilities, and established operational history. Originally designed for long-haul passenger services, the aircraft offers the dimensions and performance needed for demanding cargo operations. Converting an existing passenger aircraft also lets operators add capacity without waiting for a completely new aircraft to be manufactured.

However, the supply of suitable aircraft is becoming a major limitation. Passenger airlines are keeping their 777-300ERs in service longer, partly because delays affecting replacement 777X aircraft have disrupted fleet-renewal plans. This means conversion specialists must compete for a relatively small number of aircraft that are young enough, structurally sound, and economically viable to modify. The question is whether the remaining supply can support the ambitions of the companies developing these conversion programs.

Three companies have emerged as the principal players in the 777 conversion market: Israel Aerospace Industries (IAI), Mammoth Freighters, and Kansas Modification Center (KMC). Although all three target the same broad market, their programs involve different 777 variants, engineering approaches, and development schedules. Their progress also illustrates how quickly the industry is trying to expand conversion capacity before suitable passenger aircraft become even harder to source.

IAI was the first to establish a commercial operation with its 777-300ERSF, also known as the Big Twin. The aircraft entered commercial service in September 2025, with operators including Kalitta Air, Fly Meta and Challenge Group. Mammoth Freighters subsequently secured Federal Aviation Aviation (FAA) certification for its 777-200LRMF in April and delivered the first converted aircraft to Jetran on August 21 for DHL Air UK operations. The company is also developing the larger 777-300ERMF. Meanwhile, KMC is pursuing its 777-300ERCF, which uses a distinctive forward cargo-door design intended to reduce structural weight.

Commercial operations since September 2025

Developing a lightweight forward cargo-door solution

These programs are also expanding geographically. In October, IAI, Ascent Aviation Services and World Star Aviation announced the commencement of 777-300ERSF conversions in North America, with the first aircraft arriving at Ascent's Marana facility in Arizona. The site is expected to operate two parallel conversion lines. Mammoth has also expanded into China through a partnership with STAECO, where the first 777-200LR entered conversion last month. A second line is planned for 2027.

These investments show confidence in the converted 777 market, but adding conversion lines does not automatically increase the number of available aircraft. Companies still need to acquire passenger jets, and customers must be willing to pay substantial sums to secure them.

Boeing is pushing hard to get its answer to the new generation of cargo aircraft out of the factory. However, the 777X delays have trapped its promise

The financial appeal of a passenger-to-freighter conversion is straightforward: an operator buys an existing aircraft, modifies its interior and structure, and puts it into cargo service without paying for a brand-new production freighter. However, the purchase price represents only one part of the investment. The aircraft must undergo extensive structural modifications, maintenance inspections, and potentially expensive engine work before it can begin earning revenue.

According to aircraft consultancy IBA, a half-life 777-300ER converted into a freighter can cost approximately $75 million to $80 million, including the aircraft and conversion work. The investment can approach $100 million if the GE90-115 engines require shop visits. These costs are high, particularly when the resulting aircraft retains the operating characteristics of an older passenger jet rather than incorporating the latest generation of engines and aerodynamic improvements. The aircraft must also have sufficient remaining structural life to justify the expenditure.

Fuel consumption becomes particularly important when operators deploy converted freighters on long-distance routes with high utilization. At the TIACA Air Cargo Forum, Airbus freighter-marketing executive Bernard De L'Estoile described conversions as only part of the solution, arguing that fuel burn matters most when converted aircraft operate frequently over long distances,Air Cargo News reported. His comments should be read in the context of Airbus selling a competing new-build freighter, but the underlying economic issue matters regardless of the manufacturer's commercial interests.

The difference becomes clearer when comparing the Mammoth 777-200LRMF with the next-generation alternatives:

A converted 777 can remain attractive on routes where operators need substantial capacity but cannot justify the investment in a new aircraft. Its economics become less convincing when it flies long-haul services almost continuously, where small differences in fuel consumption accumulate over hundreds of operating hours. The commercial sweet spot is consequently likely to be routes with moderate utilization, not the most demanding intercontinental cargo networks. But if converted aircraft aren't the best long-term option for those networks, what will replace them?

The long-term answer lies in purpose-built freighters designed around modern engines, improved aerodynamics, and dedicated cargo operations. Airbus and Boeing are both developing aircraft to replace older widebody freighters, but their delivery schedules mean converted 777s will remain relevant during the transition. The A350F is currently further along in its development program, while the 777-8F is expected to arrive later in the decade.

Airbus achieved a major milestone on September 29, when the A350F completed its maiden flight. The aircraft flew for four hours and 10 minutes, beginning a flight-test campaign expected to last more than nine months. Airbus is targeting initial deliveries in the second half of 2027, although some industry observers believe the program could slip into 2028. The manufacturer had secured 115 firm orders from 14 disclosed customers by the end of August. The A350F offers a maximum structural payload of 111 tonnes and a range of 4,700 nautical miles, with Rolls-Royce Trent XWB-97 engines.

Boeing's competing 777-8F is designed to carry even more cargo, with a maximum structural payload of approximately 118.2 tonnes and a range of 5,000 nautical miles. Based on the 777X family, it incorporates a composite wing and GE9X engines, offering improved efficiency compared with the current-generation 777F. Boeing also advertises 17% more cargo volume than the existing 777F. However, the program has experienced delays, and the manufacturer is targeting deliveries around 2029, approximately two years after the expected first delivery of the 777-9. Boeing had 81 firm 777-8F orders according to the October industry comparison.

The transition will not happen overnight. Even after the first A350Fs and 777-8Fs enter service, production will need time to ramp up, and operators will need time to integrate the aircraft into their networks. That is why converted 777s have an important window to generate revenue, but the length of that window depends on whether the new-build programs meet their delivery commitments. Another complication is that the existing 777F production line has received additional time, potentially changing conversion demand.

Cargo flats are getting older, and the end is getting further out of sight. With delays more common than ever, when will cargo operators modernize?

Passenger-aircraft availability is becoming the biggest constraint on the conversion market. Strong demand for the Boeing 777-300ER has kept residual values relatively high, while 777X delays have encouraged passenger airlines to retain their existing widebody fleets. Some carriers have even invested in additional cabin refurbishments rather than retiring aircraft they originally expected to replace.

This situation creates competition between passenger airlines and freighter conversion companies. A 777-300ER that remains commercially useful in passenger service may generate more value for its existing operator than selling it to a lessor or conversion specialist. The aircraft's age is not necessarily a reason for retirement, particularly when replacement capacity is unavailable. As a result, conversion companies cannot simply purchase as many aircraft as their production facilities can accommodate. They must wait for suitable jets to become available and compete with other buyers.

Boeing is also developing its own freighter production strategy. The FAA granted Boeing an exemption allowing up to 35 additional 777Fs to receive their first airworthiness certificates between January 1, 2028, and January 1, 2031. This provides a temporary extension to the existing production program while operators await the 777-8F. According to Cirium's October 2026 analysis, the extension could influence conversion demand because some customers were waiting for clarity on continued factory-built 777F availability before making purchasing decisions.

However, the waiver does not eliminate the feedstock problem. Cirium reported 86 firm orders across the IAI, Mammoth and KMC conversion programs, while noting that securing individual aircraft for all those commitments remains challenging. The additional 777F production also represents a limited extension, not an unlimited source of new capacity.

The implications are significant for lessors and operators. If used 777 prices continue to rise, conversion costs could exceed what customers are willing to pay, especially when engine maintenance is included. Meanwhile, the A350F and 777-8F will eventually offer more attractive operating economics for airlines flying intensive long-haul networks. The remaining question is whether conversion specialists can deliver enough aircraft before feedstock scarcity and new-build competition make further expansion increasingly difficult.

The 777 conversion market is entering a period in which demand alone will not determine its success. Cargo operators still need large widebody aircraft, and converting existing passenger jets can remain economically attractive in specific markets. However, the availability of suitable airframes, the cost of engine maintenance and the operating efficiency of older aircraft will increasingly determine which conversion programs can remain competitive. The industry is facing a rather unusual situation: it needs more converted 777s, but the aircraft required to produce them are becoming more valuable to their existing owners.

Upcoming milestones include the A350F's anticipated first deliveries in the second half of 2027 and the 777-8F's expected arrival around 2029. In the meantime, IAI, Mammoth Freighters and KMC will need to secure additional passenger aircraft while managing conversion costs and production schedules. The FAA's allowance for 35 additional 777Fs also gives cargo operators another option during the transition, potentially moderating demand for converted aircraft.

Converted 777s will remain an important part of the global freighter market, but they are unlikely to be the industry's permanent answer to the shortage of large cargo aircraft. Their greatest value may lie in connecting two generations of freighters, providing capacity while manufacturers work through development and production challenges. Whether this bridge can be extended will depend less on how many conversion facilities open and more on how many suitable passenger 777s are available for conversion.

27 Years Later, Science-Fiction's Groundbreaking 4-Part Saga Is Free to Watch on Streaming

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

The machines are winning, but at least the movies are free

Some science fiction franchises are so ambitious they become their own cinematic languages. Star Wars built an entire galaxy with a mythology that’s still growing almost 50 years later, while Blade Runner helped define the vision of a far-future Los Angeles through rampant tech advancement and unchecked capitalism. They’re the kinds of franchises that can change the way we imagine the future, influencing everything from fashion and technology to the stories other filmmakers want to tell. Plenty of franchises have tried to capture that same vibe, but few have managed to pull it off quite like The Matrix.

Released in 1999, Lana and Lilly Wachowski’s cyberpunk action thriller introduced one of the most original sci-fi concepts in modern cinema (by shamelessly ripping off cyberpunk literature and golden age anime). The story follows Thomas Anderson (Keanu Reeves), a computer programmer and net surfer living under the hacker alias "Neo," who discovers that the everyday world of the late 1990s is just a complex simulation called the Matrix. Built by sentient machines, this fake reality keeps humanity docile and unconscious while harvesting their bioelectric energy in a real-world wasteland. Neo violently wakes up to the truth, joining forces with a mysterious rebel leader in Morpheus (Laurence Fishburne) and a skilled operative named Trinity (Carrie-Anne Moss). With the entire four-movie saga currently streaming free on Pluto TV, what better way to spend a weekend than by delving back into the digital rabbit hole that changed cinema forever?

Landing on the doorstep of the new millennium, The Matrix hit upon the exact cultural fears and anxieties surrounding the internet at the time. On the cusp of the impending Y2K bug, a widely feared computer glitch that threatened to disrupt everything from banking systems to power grids when the calendar rolled into 2000, the Wachowskis captured a palpable dread about future technologies, transforming fears of rogue code and automated systems into a leather-clad action movie that completely rewired 2000s sci-fi and action cinema. It’s no surprise that The Matrix became the blueprint for a whole decade of internet-obsessed action-thrillers, from Kurt Wimmer’s Equilibrium to Michael Bay’s The Island.

Despite striking gold, the Wachowskis weren't working from a blank slate. Mamoru Oshii's 1995 masterpiece Ghost in the Shell shaped the film's aesthetic and ideas, with the Wachowskis even reportedly showing the film to producer Joel Silver while pitching their vision. The Matrix inevitably combined those influences with Western cyberpunk, religious imagery, and martial arts cinema to create a singular blend of philosophical science fiction and blockbuster spectacle unlike any other. Its famous bullet-time sequences became a sensation, with the iconic rooftop showdown between Agent Smith and Neo still one of its slickest. It was visual storytelling at its finest, physically demonstrating that Neo was finally beginning to understand the rules of the simulation as he surpassed the boundaries of his human limitations.

The original film's breakout success led to The Matrix Reloaded and The Matrix Revolutions, which expanded the mythology into a multi-layered epic exploring the political machinery of Zion. The sequels might not have captured the same cultural lightning as their predecessor, but they deserve credit for refusing to simply repeat the first film's formula. Reloaded introduced more complicated questions about the cyclical nature of the Matrix, while Revolutions brought Neo's conflict with the machines to an appropriately operatic conclusion. The franchise also expanded into the digital realm with The Animatrix, a nine-part anime anthology featuring legendary Japanese directors, as well as assorted video games, and other multimedia projects, creating a sprawling sci-fi universe that showed little signs of dying.

Eighteen years after Revolutions brought Neo’s journey to a definitive close, The Matrix seemingly still had stories left to tell with 2021’s The Matrix Resurrections. Directed solely by Lana Wachowski, the fourth installment reunited Reeves and Carrie-Anne Moss, but its greatest ambition also proved to be its biggest stumbling block. Rather than delivering another nostalgic action spectacle, Resurrections tried to tackle Hollywood’s relentless obsession with reboots and legacy sequels through a self-referential lens. It was a hard sell for fans hoping for another revolutionary sci-fi leap, with the film struggling to strike a satisfying balance between its cerebral commentary and the blockbuster storytelling that made the 1999 film a masterpiece.

Still, an uneven fourth installment can't erase what The Matrix accomplished. Its vision of artificial intelligence and simulated reality feels particularly relevant today, even if we're still waiting for someone to invent a pair of sunglasses that look quite as cool. For anyone who missed the phenomenon the first time around, or simply wants to revisit one of Hollywood's most influential sci-fi universes, there's never been a better excuse to follow the white rabbit again.

Magnite vs. Pinterest: Which Media Stock Is a Better Buy in 2026?

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

One operates the ad-tech backbone for streaming; the other has built a massive online platform. Their financial profiles and risk exposures also tell very different stories.

As digital advertising shifts toward streaming and social commerce, choosing between Magnite (MGNI -0.47%) and Pinterest (PINS -4.19%) requires a close look at where marketing dollars are moving today.

Magnite serves as the technical backbone for publishers looking to sell ad space, while Pinterest provides a visual search environment where users actively look for inspiration and products. Comparing these two companies helps investors understand the different ways to play the ongoing evolution of the digital ad market.

Magnite provides a sell-side advertising platform for publishers and media owners in the media stocks landscape. As the largest independent player in this space, it helps content creators monetize their inventory across connected television, mobile, and desktop. The company maintains critical partnerships with providers like LG Ad Solutions and Telly, allowing programmatic buyers, including The Trade Desk, to access premium video inventory.

In FY 2025, Magnite reported revenue of nearly $714 million, representing a growth rate of approximately 6.9% compared to the previous year. The company achieved a net income of roughly $144.6 million during this period. This is an increase from the net income of approximately $22.8 million reported in FY 2024, reflecting an expansion in its net margin to roughly 20.3%.

Looking further into the financials, Magnite has total cash of $392.8 million and total debt of $411.9 million. Its operating cash flow for the 12-month trailing period is nearly $300 million.

Pinterest operates a visual discovery and shopping platform that now serves approximately 661 million monthly active users. Unlike traditional social media, the platform focuses on curation, making it highly attractive to advertisers in the retail and consumer packaged goods sectors. By integrating shopping features directly into its pins, the company has transformed from a simple image board into a performance-driven marketing channel.

In FY 2025, revenue reached approximately $4.2 billion, which was an increase of nearly 15.8% over the previous year. The company reported a net income of roughly $416.9 million for the year. This resulted in a net margin of approximately 9.9%, which followed a net margin of roughly 51% in FY 2024 when the company benefited from significant tax adjustments and valuation changes.

For a quick glimpse at the financials, Pinterest has $1.4 billion in total cash and $1.2 billion in total debt. Its operating cash flow for the 12-month trailing period is $1.3 billion.

Magnite faces intense competition from larger, better-funded tech giants such as Alphabet, Amazon (AMZN -0.35%), and Comcast (CMCSA -2.24%). The company is also navigating ongoing litigation with Alphabet regarding ad-tech practices, which could cause operational distractions or financial pressure. Technological shifts, including the removal of third-party identifiers and the rise of new privacy regulations, continue to challenge the way Magnite identifies and targets audiences for its publishing partners.

Pinterest faces significant competition for user attention and advertising budgets from Amazon, Meta Platforms, Alphabet, and smaller rivals like Snap (SNAP -2.55%) and Reddit (RDDT +1.38%). The company is currently involved in a securities class action lawsuit related to previous advertising disclosures. Furthermore, Pinterest remains vulnerable to changes in mobile operating systems that limit tracking capabilities, which can make it harder for advertisers to measure the effectiveness of their campaigns.

Pinterest appears to be more of a value, according to traditional metrics.

Pinterest has built up an impressive monthly user base. And there is something to be said for building your own network and controlling that user base. But Pinterest is caught between being a social media site and an advertiser, whereas Magnite's value is more straightforward. Also, between the two, Magnite is more of a growth stock, whereas Pinterest is seeking more stability, with its stock price down 30% over the last 12 months. In comparison, the Magnite stock price is up 81.3% over the same period.

Analysts also have a more favorable outlook on Magnite: of the 18 analysts who rate Magnite, 89% rate it a buy, with 11% rating it a hold. In comparison, the view is more split on Pinterest. Of the 42 analysts who rate PINS, 50% rate it a buy and 50% a hold.

As a growth stock, Magnite could be hit particularly hard if there were a slowdown in the economy. And heading into 2027, we're seeing concerns about an artificial intelligence bubble and mixed signals about the economy's strength. Still, between the two, I would rather own Magnite as a long-term position because of its sales growth opportunities.

Jack Delaney has no position in any of the stocks mentioned. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends Alphabet, Amazon, Meta Platforms, Pinterest, Reddit, and The Trade Desk. The Motley Fool recommends Comcast and Magnite. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

Palantir vs. Snowflake: The AI Sell-Off Is Exposing a Critical Difference for 2027

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Palantir and Snowflake continue to deliver impressive growth, but soaring AI expectations and renewed market volatility are putting their valuations under scrutiny.

The artificial intelligence (AI) boom has fueled remarkable growth across the technology sector, but the recent sell-off has Wall Street reconsidering how much that growth is worth. For investors looking toward 2027, the pullback presents an opportunity to reassess two leading enterprise AI players: Palantir Technologies (PLTR +3.48%) and Snowflake (SNOW +2.35%).

Both of these companies are benefiting from growing AI adoption, but their distinct business models, financial performance, and valuations present nuanced investment opportunities.

Palantir develops software platforms that help government agencies and businesses integrate complex datasets, improve decision-making, and deploy artificial intelligence. Its Gotham, Foundry, and Artificial Intelligence Platform (AIP) products serve customers ranging from the U.S. military to commercial enterprises.

In fiscal 2025, Palantir generated $4.5 billion in revenue, an increase of 56% year over year. Net income reached approximately $1.6 billion, representing a profit margin of 36.5%. More recent results demonstrate accelerating growth. In the third quarter of 2026, revenue surged 96% to $2.3 billion, while U.S. commercial revenue climbed 148%. Notably, U.S. commercial revenue surpassed U.S. government revenue for the first time, highlighting the company's expanding presence in the private sector.

At the end of 2025, the company carried virtually no debt, with a current ratio of approximately 7.1x. Free cash flow reached $2.1 billion during the year, providing substantial flexibility to fund continued expansion. However, stock-based compensation totaled approximately $684 million, representing 32% of operating cash flow, which could introduce potential shareholder dilution despite the company's strong cash generation.

Snowflake operates a cloud-based data platform that enables businesses to store, manage, and analyze information across multiple cloud environments. Its consumption-based pricing model allows customers to pay for the computing resources they use, while partnerships with Amazon (AMZN -0.35%), Microsoft (MSFT -0.99%), and Alphabet (GOOGL +0.78%) (GOOG +0.78%) support its global reach. As businesses increasingly incorporate artificial intelligence into their operations, Snowflake's platform plays an important role in making enterprise data accessible for AI applications.In fiscal 2026, Snowflake generated approximately $4.7 billion in revenue, an increase of 29% year over year. Still, it was unable to turn a profit and reported a net loss of $1.3 billion, representing a negative profit margin of roughly 28%. However, more recent results demonstrate continued growth. In the second quarter of fiscal 2027, revenue increased 35% to $1.55 billion, while product revenue climbed 37%.

At the end of fiscal 2026, its debt-to-equity ratio stood near 1.2x, while its current ratio was approximately 1.3x. The company generated $1.1 billion in free cash flow, but stock-based compensation totaled $1.6 billion, exceeding operating cash flow.

One of the key risks for Palantir comes from its reliance on large government and enterprise contracts, where lengthy sales cycles and customer concentration can create revenue uncertainty. In addition, its involvement in sensitive defense and surveillance operations also introduces regulatory and reputational risks.

Top risks for Snowflake include intense competition and a consumption-based revenue model that makes growth sensitive to customer spending. Not to mention, ever-growing cybersecurity threats introduce operational and regulatory risks.

Snowflake offers a lower P/S ratio, measuring price against sales over the past twelve months, while Palantir is cheaper relative to future earnings estimates via its Forward P/E.

Valuation metrics include sourcing from Financial Modeling Prep (FMP) and may differ from other data providers.

From a valuation standpoint, neither stock is cheap. Palantir trades at 103 times forward earnings, while Snowflake commands an even higher multiple of 131 times. Snowflake is considerably cheaper on a price-to-sales basis, at 18.9 times sales compared with Palantir's 54.5 times, but its continued losses and path toward sustained GAAP profitability are less certain.The key difference is that Palantir has demonstrated a stronger ability to turn growing AI adoption into profits. Its latest quarterly revenue surged 96%, driven by accelerating commercial demand, while its strong balance sheet and minimal debt provide considerable financial flexibility.Looking ahead to 2027, Palantir is my preferred buy, although its valuation gives me pause. Investors are paying a substantial premium for future growth, leaving little room for disappointment if the company falls short of expectations. Ultimately, both stocks carry lofty valuations, and the recent sell-off doesn't automatically make either a bargain.

RJ Fulton has no position in any of the stocks mentioned. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends Alphabet, Amazon, Microsoft, Palantir Technologies, and Snowflake. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.
